=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two integers n and m that consist of the same number of digits.
You can perform the following operations any number of times:

Choose any digit from n that is not 9 and increase it by 1.
Choose any digit from n that is not 0 and decrease it by 1.

The integer n must not be a prime number at any point, including its original value and after each operation.
The cost of a transformation is the sum of all values that n takes throughout the operations performed.
Return the minimum cost to transform n into m. If it is impossible, return -1.
 
Example 1:

Input: n = 10, m = 12
Output: 85
Explanation:
We perform the following operations:

Increase the first digit, now n = 20.
Increase the second digit, now n = 21.
Increase the second digit, now n = 22.
Decrease the first digit, now n = 12.


Example 2:

Input: n = 4, m = 8
Output: -1
Explanation:
It is impossible to make n equal to m.

Example 3:

Input: n = 6, m = 2
Output: -1
Explanation: 
Since 2 is already a prime, we can't make n equal to m.

 
Constraints:

1 <= n, m < 10^4
n and m consist of the same number of digits.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minOperations(self, n: int, m: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

On-the-fly neighbor generation:
def neighbors(x):
    s = str(x)
    # if d maybe len(s) = d because x in range
    for i, ch in enumerate(s):
        digit = ord(ch)-48
        if digit < 9:
            # increment
            # if i==0 and digit==0? not possible for leading; if leading digit 0 not in range
            new = x + 10**(d-1-i)
            if not is_prime[new]:
                yield new
        if digit > 0:
            # decrement
            # if i==0 and digit==1: would lead to leading zero; skip
            if i == 0 and digit == 1:
                continue
            new = x - 10**(d-1-i)
            if not is_prime[new]:
                yield new
But if d=1 and i=0, digit=1, skip decrement to0. If we wanted allow 0, but no need. If digit=0 at leading? not in range. For non-leading, decrement okay. If digit=0, decrement not allowed. If digit=9, increment not allowed.

Alternatively, generate by modifying string:
for pos in range(d):
    for delta in (-1,1):
       if valid digit...
But computing x + delta*place.

Need ensure new remains in range start..end. For leading increment if digit=9 skip; if digit<9, new < end. For leading decrement skip if digit=1; if digit>1, new >= start. For non-leading, new remains in range. Good.

Dijkstra:
import heapq
def minOperations(self,n,m):
    if is_prime[n] or is_prime[m]: return -1  # But if n=m? if prime return -1. If m prime impossible. If n prime original invalid.
    d=len(str(n))
    start=10**(d-1)
    end=10**d -1
    INF=10**18
    dist=[INF]*(end+1?) Could allocate 10000.
    dist[n]=n
    pq=[(n,n)]
    while pq:
       cost,x=heappop
       if cost!=dist[x]: continue
       if x==m: return cost
       for y in neighbors(x):
           nd=cost+y
           if nd<dist[y]:
              dist[y]=nd; heappush
    return -1

Need if n=m and composite: dist[n]=n, pq pops, x==m return n. Good. If n=m prime, initial check returns -1. If m prime but n composite, initial check returns -1. If n prime but m composite, -1. If n=m=1, is_prime[1]=False, return 1. Is 1 prime? no. Good.

But wait, if n is prime, original value invalid, so impossible even if n=m. Return -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a path from n=100,m=190 that goes through 100->110->120->130->140->150->160->170->180->190, all composite. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a path from n=100,m=190 that goes through 100->110->120->130->140->150->160->170->180->190, all composite. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a path from n=100,m=190 that goes
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import heapq

class Solution:
    def minOperations(self, n: int, m: int) -> int:
        # Sieve up to 9999 because n, m < 10^4
        MAX = 10000
        is_prime = [True] * MAX
        is_prime[0] = is_prime[1] = False
        for i in range(2, 100):
            if is_prime[i]:
                for j in range(i * i, MAX, i):
                    is_prime[j] = False

        # Original and final values must not be prime
        if is_prime[n] or is_prime[m]:
            return -1

        d = len(str(n))
        start = 10 ** (d - 1)
        end = 10 ** d - 1

        INF = 10 ** 18
        dist = [INF] * MAX
        dist[n] = n

        pq = [(n, n)]

        while pq:
            cost, x = heapq.heappop(pq)
            if cost != dist[x]:
                continue
            if x == m:
                return cost

            s = str(x)
            for i, ch in enumerate(s):
                digit = ord(ch) - 48
                place = 10 ** (d - 1 - i)

                # Increase digit by 1
                if digit < 9:
                    y = x + place
                    if not is_prime[y] and cost + y < dist[y]:
                        dist[y] = cost + y
                        heapq.heappush(pq, (dist[y], y))

                # Decrease digit by 1
                if digit > 0:
                    # Do not let the leading digit become 0,
                    # otherwise the number would lose a digit.
                    if i == 0 and digit == 1:
                        continue
                    y = x - place
                    if not is_prime[y] and cost + y < dist[y]:
                        dist[y] = cost + y
                        heapq.heappush(pq, (dist[y], y))

        return -1
```
